The following description:

The satp CSR is an SXLEN-bit read/write register which controls supervisor-mode address translation and protection. It is formatted as shown in Figure 63 for SXLEN=32 and Figure 64 for SXLEN=64.

*   **SXLEN=32 Register Layout (Figure 63):**
    *   Bit 31: MODE (WARL)
    *   Bits 30-22: ASID (WARL)
    *   Bits 21-0: PPN (WARL)
*   **SXLEN=64 Register Layout (Figure 64):**
    *   Bit 63: MODE (WARL)
    *   Bits 60-44: ASID (WARL)
    *   Bits 43-0: PPN (WARL)

**Register Fields and Functions:**
*   The satp.PPN field holds the physical page number (PPN) of the root page table, which is the supervisor physical address divided by 4 KiB.
*   Storing a PPN in satp, rather than a physical address, supports a physical address space larger than 4 GiB for RV32.
*   The satp.PPN field might not be capable of holding all physical page numbers.
*   Some platform standards might place constraints on the values satp.PPN may assume, e.g., by requiring that all physical page numbers corresponding to main memory be representable.
*   The satp.ASID field holds an address space identifier (ASID), which facilitates address-translation fences on a per-addressspace basis.
*   The satp.MODE field selects the current address-translation scheme.
*   The ASID and the page table base address are stored in the same CSR to allow the pair to be changed atomically on a context switch.

**MODE Field Encodings (Table 40):**

*   **For SXLEN=32:**
    *   If MODE = 0 1 (Bare Sv32): No translation or protection. Page-based 32-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.3).
    *   The only other valid setting for MODE is Sv32 (paged virtual-memory scheme described in Section 12.3).

*   **For SXLEN=64:**
    *   If MODE = 0 (Bare): No translation or protection.
    *   If MODE = 1-7: Reserved for standard use.
    *   If MODE = 8 (Sv39): Page-based 39-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.4).
    *   If MODE = 9 (Sv48): Page-based 48-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.5).
    *   If MODE = 10 (Sv57): Page-based 57-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.6).
    *   If MODE = 11 (Sv64): Reserved for page-based 64-bit virtual addressing.
    *   If MODE = 12-13: Reserved for standard use.
    *   If MODE = 14-15: Designated for custom use.

**Specific Behavioral Rules:**
*   **When MODE=Bare:** Supervisor virtual addresses are equal to supervisor physical addresses, and there is no additional memory protection beyond the physical memory protection scheme described in Section 3.7.
*   **To select MODE=Bare (SXLEN=32):** Software MUST write zero to the remaining fields of satp (bits 30-0).
*   **To select MODE=Bare (SXLEN=64):** Software MUST write zero to the remaining fields of satp (bits 59-0).
*   **Attempting to select MODE=Bare with a nonzero pattern in the remaining fields:** Has an UNSPECIFIED effect on the value that the remaining fields assume and an UNSPECIFIED effect on address translation and protection behavior.
*   **When SXLEN=32 and MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]=3:** These encodings are designated for custom use.
*   **When SXLEN=32 and MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]≠3:** These encodings are reserved for future standard use.
*   **When SXLEN=64 and MODE=Bare:** All satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare are reserved for future standard use.
*   **When SXLEN=64 and MODE settings are reserved for future use:** They may define different interpretations of the other fields in satp.
*   **If satp is written with an unsupported MODE:** The entire write has no effect; no fields in satp are modified.

**ASIDLEN and ASIDMAX:**
*   The number of ASID bits is UNSPECIFIED and may be zero.
*   The number of implemented ASID bits (ASIDLEN) may be determined by writing one to every bit position in the ASID field, then reading back the value in satp to see which bit positions in the ASID field hold a one.
*   The least-significant bits of ASID are implemented first: if ASIDLEN > 0, ASID[ASIDLEN-1:0] is writable.
*   The maximal value of ASIDLEN (ASIDMAX) is 9 for Sv32 or 16 for Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57.

**Activity and Ordering:**
*   The satp CSR is considered active when the effective privilege mode is S-mode or U-mode.
*   Executions of the address-translation algorithm may only begin using a given value of satp when satp is active.
*   Translations that began while satp was active are not required to complete or terminate when satp is no longer active, unless an SFENCE.VMA instruction matching the address and ASID is executed.
*   Writing satp does not imply any ordering constraints between page-table updates and subsequent address translations.
*   Writing satp does not imply any invalidation of address-translation caches.
*   If the new address space's page tables have been modified, or if an ASID is reused, it may be necessary to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction after, or in some cases before, writing satp.

**Table 40. Encoding of satp MODE field.**

| SXLEN=32 | SXLEN=32 | SXLEN=32 |
|---|---|---|
| Value | Name | Description |
| 0 1 | Bare Sv32 | No translation or protection. Page-based 32-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.3). |
| SXLEN=64 | SXLEN=64 | SXLEN=64 |
| Value | Name | Description |
| 0 | Bare | No translation or protection. |
| 1-7 | - | Reserved for standard use |
| 8 | Sv39 | Page-based 39-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.4). |
| 9 | Sv48 | Page-based 48-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.5). |
| 10 | Sv57 | Page-based 57-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.6). |
| 11 | Sv64 | Reserved for page-based 64-bit virtual addressing. |
| 12-13 | - | Reserved for standard use |
| 14-15 | - | Designated for custom use |
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The satp CSR is an SXLEN-bit read/write register which controls supervisor-mode address translation and protection.
RULE 2: For SXLEN=32, the satp register consists of a MODE field at bit 31 (WARL), an ASID field at bits 30-22 (WARL), and a PPN field at bits 21-0 (WARL).
RULE 3: For SXLEN=64, the satp register consists of a MODE field at bit 63 (WARL), an ASID field at bits 60-44 (WARL), and a PPN field at bits 43-0 (WARL).
RULE 4: The satp.PPN field holds the physical page number (PPN) of the root page table, which is the supervisor physical address divided by 4 KiB.
RULE 5: Storing a PPN in the satp register, rather than a physical address, supports a physical address space larger than 4 GiB for RV32.
RULE 6: The satp.PPN field might not be capable of holding all physical page numbers.
RULE 7: Some platform standards might place constraints on the values satp.PPN may assume, such as requiring that all physical page numbers corresponding to main memory be representable.
RULE 8: The satp.ASID field holds an address space identifier (ASID), which facilitates address-translation fences on a per-addressspace basis.
RULE 9: The satp.MODE field selects the current address-translation scheme.
RULE 10: The ASID and the page table base address are stored in the same satp CSR to allow the pair to be changed atomically on a context switch.
RULE 11: For SXLEN=32, if the satp.MODE field is 0 1, it represents Bare Sv32, which provides no translation or protection and page-based 32-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 12: For SXLEN=32, the only other valid setting for the satp.MODE field is Sv32, which is a paged virtual-memory scheme.
RULE 13: For SXLEN=64, if the satp.MODE field is 0, it represents Bare, which provides no translation or protection.
RULE 14: For SXLEN=64, if the satp.MODE field is 1-7, the setting is reserved for standard use.
RULE 15: For SXLEN=64, if the satp.MODE field is 8, it represents Sv39, which is page-based 39-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 16: For SXLEN=64, if the satp.MODE field is 9, it represents Sv48, which is page-based 48-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 17: For SXLEN=64, if the satp.MODE field is 10, it represents Sv57, which is page-based 57-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 18: For SXLEN=64, if the satp.MODE field is 11, it is reserved for page-based 64-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 19: For SXLEN=64, if the satp.MODE field is 12-13, the setting is reserved for standard use.
RULE 20: For SXLEN=64, if the satp.MODE field is 14-15, the setting is designated for custom use.
RULE 21: When the satp.MODE field is set to Bare, supervisor virtual addresses are equal to supervisor physical addresses, and there is no additional memory protection beyond the physical memory protection scheme.
RULE 22: To select MODE=Bare for SXLEN=32, software MUST write zero to the remaining fields of satp (bits 30-0).
RULE 23: To select MODE=Bare for SXLEN=64, software MUST write zero to the remaining fields of satp (bits 59-0).
RULE 24: Attempting to select MODE=Bare with a nonzero pattern in the remaining fields of satp has an UNSPECIFIED effect on the value that the remaining fields assume and an UNSPECIFIED effect on address translation and protection behavior.
RULE 25: When SXLEN=32, satp.MODE is Bare, and ASID[8:7]=3, these encodings are designated for custom use.
RULE 26: When SXLEN=32, satp.MODE is Bare, and ASID[8:7]≠3, these encodings are reserved for future standard use.
RULE 27: When SXLEN=64 and the satp.MODE field is Bare, all satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare are reserved for future standard use.
RULE 28: When SXLEN=64 and the satp.MODE field settings are reserved for future use, they may define different interpretations of the other fields in satp.
RULE 29: If satp is written with an unsupported MODE, the entire write has no effect and no fields in satp are modified.
RULE 30: The number of ASID bits in the satp register is UNSPECIFIED and may be zero.
RULE 31: The number of implemented ASID bits (ASIDLEN) may be determined by writing one to every bit position in the ASID field of satp, then reading back the value in satp to see which bit positions in the ASID field hold a one.
RULE 32: If ASIDLEN > 0, the least-significant bits of the ASID field in satp (ASID[ASIDLEN-1:0]) are writable, as the least-significant bits of ASID are implemented first.
RULE 33: The maximal value of ASIDLEN (ASIDMAX) is 9 for Sv32 and 16 for Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57 in the satp register.
RULE 34: The satp CSR is considered active when the effective privilege mode is S-mode or U-mode.
RULE 35: Executions of the address-translation algorithm may only begin using a given value of satp when satp is active.
RULE 36: Translations that began while satp was active are not required to complete or terminate when satp is no longer active, unless an SFENCE.VMA instruction matching the address and ASID is executed.
RULE 37: Writing satp does not imply any ordering constraints between page-table updates and subsequent address translations.
RULE 38: Writing satp does not imply any invalidation of address-translation caches.
RULE 39: If the new address space's page tables have been modified, or if an ASID is reused, it may be necessary to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction after, or in some cases before, writing satp.
======================================================= END - 54 =======================================================

The following description:

Implementations MUST only perform implicit reads of the translation data structures pointed to by the current contents of the satp register or a subsequent valid (V=1) translation data structure entry, and must only raise exceptions for implicit accesses that are generated as a result of instruction execution, not those that are performed speculatively.

Changing satp.MODE from Bare to other modes and vice versa also takes effect immediately, without the need to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction.

Likewise, changes to satp.ASID take effect immediately.

When software recycles an ASID (i.e., reassociates it with a different page table), it SHOULD first change satp to point to the new page table using the recycled ASID, then execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1= x0 and rs2 set to the recycled ASID.

Alternatively, software can execute the same SFENCE.VMA instruction while a different ASID is loaded into satp, provided the next time satp is loaded with the recycled ASID, it is simultaneously loaded with the new page table.

If the implementation does not provide ASIDs, or software chooses to always use ASID 0, then after every satp write, software SHOULD execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1= x0.

For implementations that make satp.MODE read-only zero (always Bare), attempts to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction might raise an illegal-instruction exception.

If a leaf PTE is modified and the corresponding virtual address is accessed without a subsuming SFENCE.VMA having been executed in between, then either the new translation or any older translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA was executed will be used. It is unpredictable which translation will be chosen from that set, and subsequent accesses to the same virtual address might use different translations from that set. This property applies even if the virtual-address width for that translation differs from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: Implementations MUST only perform implicit reads of the translation data structures pointed to by the current contents of the satp register or a subsequent valid (V=1) translation data structure entry.
RULE 2: Implementations MUST only raise exceptions for implicit accesses that are generated as a result of instruction execution, not those that are performed speculatively, for translation data structures pointed to by the current contents of the satp register or a subsequent valid (V=1) translation data structure entry.
RULE 3: Changing satp.MODE from Bare to other modes and vice versa takes effect immediately, without the need to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction.
RULE 4: Changes to satp.ASID take effect immediately.
RULE 5: When software recycles an ASID by reassociating it with a different page table, it SHOULD first change satp to point to the new page table using the recycled ASID, then execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1= x0 and rs2 set to the recycled ASID.
RULE 6: Software can execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction while a different ASID is loaded into satp, provided the next time satp is loaded with the recycled ASID, it is simultaneously loaded with the new page table.
RULE 7: If the implementation does not provide ASIDs, software SHOULD execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1= x0 after every satp write.
RULE 8: If software chooses to always use ASID 0, software SHOULD execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1= x0 after every satp write.
RULE 9: For implementations that make satp.MODE read-only zero (always Bare), attempts to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction might raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 10: If a leaf PTE is modified and the corresponding virtual address is accessed without a subsuming SFENCE.VMA having been executed in between, then either the new translation or any older translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA was executed will be used, even if the virtual-address width for that translation differs from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.
RULE 11: If a leaf PTE is modified and the corresponding virtual address is accessed without a subsuming SFENCE.VMA having been executed in between, it is unpredictable which translation will be chosen from the set of the new translation and any older translations since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA, even if the virtual-address width for that translation differs from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.
RULE 12: If a leaf PTE is modified and the corresponding virtual address is accessed without a subsuming SFENCE.VMA having been executed in between, subsequent accesses to the same virtual address might use different translations from the set of the new translation and any older translations since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA, even if the virtual-address width for that translation differs from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.

The following description:

* When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of the satp register, supervisor virtual addresses are translated into supervisor physical addresses via a two-level page table.
* When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of the satp register, the 20-bit VPN is translated into a 22-bit physical page number (PPN), while the 12-bit page offset is untranslated.
* The physical page number of the root page table is stored in the satp register.
* For an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address, when the value of satp.MODE is Sv32, a 34-bit physical address is produced directly, and therefore no zero extension is needed.
* For an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address, when the value of satp.MODE is Bare, the 32-bit virtual address is translated (unmodified) into a 32-bit physical address, and then that physical address is zero-extended into a 34-bit machine-level physical address.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of the satp register, supervisor virtual addresses are translated into supervisor physical addresses via a two-level page table.
RULE 2: When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of the satp register, the 20-bit VPN is translated into a 22-bit physical page number (PPN), while the 12-bit page offset is untranslated.
RULE 3: The physical page number of the root page table is stored in the satp register.
RULE 4: For an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address, when the value of satp.MODE is Sv32, a 34-bit physical address is produced directly, and therefore no zero extension is needed.
RULE 5: For an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address, when the value of satp.MODE is Bare, the 32-bit virtual address is translated (unmodified) into a 32-bit physical address, and then that physical address is zero-extended into a 34-bit machine-level physical address.

The following description:

The `satp` register must be active for a virtual address `va` to be translated into a physical address `pa`. The `satp` register is active if the effective privilege mode is S-mode or U-mode.

In the virtual address translation process, the value `a` is defined as `a = satp.ppn × PAGESIZE`, and `i` is defined as `LEVELS-1`. For Sv32, `PAGESIZE=2 12` and `LEVELS=2`.

The algorithm may only touch page tables reachable from the root page table indicated in `satp`. As a consequence, the range of addresses that an implementation's page-table walker will touch is fully under supervisor control.

Implementations may execute the address-translation algorithm speculatively at any time, for any virtual address, as long as `satp` is active (as defined in Section 12.1.11).
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The satp register must be active for a virtual address va to be translated into a physical address pa.
RULE 2: The satp register is active if the effective privilege mode is S-mode or U-mode.
RULE 3: In the virtual address translation process, the value a is defined as a = satp.ppn × PAGESIZE.
RULE 4: In the virtual address translation process involving the satp register, the value i is defined as LEVELS-1.
RULE 5: For Sv32, PAGESIZE=2 12 and LEVELS=2 are the values used in the definitions for a and i which involve the satp register.
RULE 6: The algorithm may only touch page tables reachable from the root page table indicated in the satp register.
RULE 7: The range of addresses that an implementation's page-table walker will touch is fully under supervisor control as a consequence of the algorithm only touching page tables reachable from the root page table indicated in the satp register.
RULE 8: Implementations may execute the address-translation algorithm speculatively at any time, for any virtual address, as long as the satp register is active.

The following description:

* `vsatp` is VS-mode's version of supervisor register `satp`.
* When V=1, `vsatp` substitutes for the usual `satp`, so instructions that normally read or modify `satp` actually access `vsatp` instead.
* When V=1, a write to `satp` with an unsupported MODE value is ignored and no write to `vsatp` is effected.
* When V=0, a write to `vsatp` with an unsupported MODE value is either ignored as it is for `satp`, or the fields of `vsatp` are treated as WARL in the normal way.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: `vsatp` is VS-mode's version of supervisor register `satp`.
RULE 2: When V=1, `vsatp` substitutes for the usual `satp` such that instructions that normally read or modify `satp` actually access `vsatp` instead.
RULE 3: When V=1, a write to `satp` with an unsupported MODE value is ignored and no write to `vsatp` is effected.
RULE 4: When V=0, a write to `vsatp` with an unsupported MODE value is either ignored as it is for `satp` or the fields of `vsatp` are treated as WARL in the normal way.
======================================================= END - 63 =======================================================

The following description:

The counter-enable (scounteren) CSR is a 32-bit register that controls the availability of the hardware performance monitoring counters to U-mode.

scounteren must be implemented.

* When the CY, TM, IR, or HPM n bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the cycle, time, instret, or hpmcountern register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
* When one of these bits is set, access to the corresponding register is permitted.
* Any of the bits may be read-only zero, indicating reads to the corresponding counter will cause an exception when executing in U-mode.
* They are effectively WARL fields.
* The setting of a bit in mcounteren does not affect whether the corresponding bit in scounteren is writable.
* U-mode may only access a counter if the corresponding bits in scounteren and mcounteren are both set.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The scounteren CSR is a 32-bit register that controls the availability of the hardware performance monitoring counters to U-mode.
RULE 2: The scounteren register must be implemented.
RULE 3: When the CY bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the cycle register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 4: When the TM bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the time register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 5: When the IR bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the instret register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 6: When the HPM n bit in the scounteren register is clear, attempts to read the hpmcountern register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 7: When the CY bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the cycle register is permitted.
RULE 8: When the TM bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the time register is permitted.
RULE 9: When the IR bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the instret register is permitted.
RULE 10: When the HPM n bit in the scounteren register is set, access to the hpmcountern register is permitted.
RULE 11: Any of the bits in the scounteren register may be read-only zero, indicating reads to the corresponding counter will cause an exception when executing in U-mode.
RULE 12: The bits in the scounteren register are effectively WARL fields.
RULE 13: The setting of a bit in mcounteren does not affect whether the corresponding bit in scounteren is writable.
RULE 14: U-mode may only access a counter if the corresponding bits in scounteren and mcounteren are both set.
